# Call log (raw)

Everything the debugging session called, generated from the archives by `exports/build_log.py`. The readable summary is `SUMMARY.md`; the full conversation export is the zip in ~/Downloads.

## A. Harness agent: every Claude call

Each call is one structured-output request to the Claude API (no tools; images attached where noted). `cached` = served from the content-hash cache, no tokens spent. Full request: `runs/<run>/<folder>/request.json`; answer: `output.json`; thinking summary: `thinking.md`.

Derived 720p is about $0.151/s and 480p about $0.07/s (inferred).","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/mini/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/us/*","price":"Priced separately, with rates on each US endpoint page. A search snippet quoted about $0.222/s at 720p with video inputs (not verified).","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/bytedance/seedance"}],"image_models_for_reference_views":[{"id":"fal-ai/nano-banana-pro/edit","why":"Google's top edit model on fal, good for cleaning a messy visitor photo (glare, crowd, glass reflections) into a clean product-style view of the same object and for 'as it looked new or alive' restorations. Supports multiple input images, up to 14 references in context, and an optional web search for grounding. This is a recommendation from fal's descriptions, not a benchmark.","inputs":"prompt (required), image_urls[], num_images 1-4, aspect_ratio (auto, 21:9 ... 1:1 ... 9:16, 4:1, 8:1, etc.), resolution 1K/2K/4K, output_format png/jpeg/webp, seed, safety_tolerance 1-6, system_prompt, enable_web_search, thinking_level high","price":"$0.15 per image. 4K is 2x. Web search adds $0.015.","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/nano-banana-pro/edit/llms.txt"},{"id":"fal-ai/nano-banana-2/edit","why":"Cheaper Nano Banana tier with the same Gemini-style editing interface. Can also be grounded with Google Image Search (enable_image_search), which helps with 'how it originally looked'.","inputs":"prompt, image_urls[] (14-reference limit), num_images 1-4, aspect_ratio, resolution 0.5K/1K/2K/4K, seed, thinking_level minimal/high, enable_web_search, enable_image_search, output_format, safety_tolerance","price":"$0.08 per image. 0.5K is 0.75x, 2K is 1.5x, 4K is 2x. Web search adds $0.015 and high thinking adds $0.002.","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/nano-banana-2/edit/llms.txt"},{"id":"fal-ai/qwen-image-edit-2511-multiple-angles","why":"Built for novel camera angles of the same object: explicit horizontal rotation, vertical angle and zoom. Suited to producing side, back, top and close-up views from a single front photo.","inputs":"image_urls[] (required), horizontal_angle 0-360 (default 0), vertical_angle -30 to 90 (default 0), zoom 0-10 (default 5), additional_prompt, lora_scale 0-4, image_size, guidance_scale 1-20 (4.5), num_inference_steps 1-50 (28), negative_prompt, seed, num_images 1-4, output_format","price":"$0.035 per megapixel","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/qwen-image-edit-2511-multiple-angles/llms.txt"},{"id":"fal-ai/flux-2-pro/edit","why":"Multi-reference editing that keeps identity consistent, and cheap per image. A good alternative for cleanup and style-consistent sets.","inputs":"prompt (required), image_urls[] (required; maximum count not stated), image_size (default auto), seed, safety_tolerance 1-5, output_format jpeg/png","price":"$0.03 for the first output megapixel plus $0.015 for each extra input or output megapixel (1024x1024 is $0.03, 1920x1080 is $0.045)","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/flux-2-pro/edit/llms.txt"},{"id":"fal-ai/bytedance/seedream/v4.5/edit","why":"ByteDance's own image model, the same family as Seedance. Supports up to 10 inputs and multi-image outputs per run (max_images), so one call can return a consistent set of views.","inputs":"image_urls[] (up to 10, required), prompt, image_size (default 2048x2048, roughly 1920-4096 per side), num_images 1-6, max_images 1-6 (inputs plus outputs 15 or fewer), seed","price":"$0.04 per image","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/bytedance/seedream/v4.5/edit/llms.txt"}],"open_questions":["Standard-tier resolution: the llms.txt schemas list 480p/720p/1080p/4k, but the fal API doc .md pages list only 480p/720p, and ByteDance says native output is 480p/720p. Is 1080p/4k native or upscaled, and is it accepted by every standard endpoint? Test this with a real call.","seed: is it a valid input? The API docs (.md) say yes; the llms.txt schemas show it only in the output. Check the fal playground or send a request with seed.","Supported speech languages: no official fal or ByteDance page lists them. English is confirmed strong only through ByteDance's benchmark categories. The 8-language list is third-party.","Narration fidelity: does Seedance speak a quoted English line word for word, including proper nouns and dates (e.g. '120 AD', place names)? Not documented, so test it. Also unknown: whether it will lay a supplied @Audio1 narration track over the clip unchanged, or only use it as a voice/style reference.","Voice consistency across two 15 s clips (for a 30 s film): no documented voice or speaker ID control. The narrator's voice may change between generations. Possible fixes: reuse the same @Audio1 voice sample, or generate with audio off and add separate TTS.","No official timecode syntax for placing lines at given seconds. How many words fit cleanly in 15 s is also untested.","Reference-to-video per-second price at 720p with images only: about $0.30/s is my inference from the token formula; fal does not state it directly. The $0.1814/s with video inputs and $3.02 per 10 s figures come from search snippets of fal pages.","Restrictions on real-person or face references and on content moderation for reference images: not covered on the fal pages fetched.","The difference between the mini and fast tiers in quality is not described by fal.","Image models: none of the sources benchmark multi-view consistency for one object. Picking between Nano Banana Pro, Qwen multiple-angles and Seedream 4.5 needs a quick side-by-side test on a real exhibit photo. A newer Seedream 5.0 may exist on fal (seen in a fal learn article, not verified)."]}
  
  Practice/community report:
  {"seedance_models":[{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","kind":"reference-to-video (omni reference, edit, extend): up to 9 images + 3 videos + 3 audio (12 files max), duration auto or 4-15 s, 480p/720p/1080p/4k, generate_audio default true","url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/fast/reference-to-video","kind":"reference-to-video, fast tier (720p max, cheaper)","url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/fast/reference-to-video"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/text-to-video","kind":"text-to-video (480p/720p, 4-15 s or auto, optional seed)","url":"https://fal.ai/docs/model-api-reference/video-generation-api/bytedance-seedance-2.0-text-to-video.md"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/image-to-video","kind":"image-to-video: image_url = start frame, optional end_image_url = end frame","url":"https://fal.ai/docs/model-api-reference/video-generation-api/bytedance-seedance-2.0-text-to-video.md"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/us/reference-to-video","kind":"US-hosted reference-to-video variant","url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/us/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/mini/reference-to-video","kind":"Mini (cheaper) reference-to-video; same prompt grammar per Runway guide","url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/mini/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"id":"bytedance/seedance-2.5/reference-to-video (also /text-to-video, /image-to-video)","kind":"Seedance 2.5: native 4-30 s in ONE pass, 480p/720p, up to 30 images/10 videos/10 audio; same @Image1 grammar as 2.0. Would remove the need to stitch two 15 s clips for the 30 s film","url":"https://fal.ai/learn/devs/seedance-2-5-vs-seedance-2-0"}],"params":[{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"prompt","values":"string, required","notes":"References are named in-prompt by upload order: @Image1..@Image9, @Video1..@Video3, @Audio1..@Audio3","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"image_urls","values":"list, up to 9; max 30 MB each; jpeg/png/webp/bmp/tiff/gif/heic","notes":"Order matters: the 1st URL is @Image1","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"video_urls","values":"list, up to 3; combined 2-15 s; <50 MB total; mp4/mov; roughly 480p-720p","notes":"Can be used to pass clip 1 as @Video1 so clip 2 extends it. Using video refs lowers the per-token rate","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"audio_urls","values":"list, up to 3; combined max 15 s; mp3/wav; max 15 MB each","notes":"Requires at least one image or video reference. This is the slot for a narrator voice-timbre reference (@Audio1)","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"duration","values":"\"auto\" (default) or \"4\"..\"15\" (string)","notes":"Use \"15\" for each half of a 30 s film","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"resolution","values":"480p, 720p (default), 1080p, 4k","notes":"fal's own how-to page says otherwise in places; check before relying on 1080p","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"aspect_ratio","values":"auto (default), 21:9, 16:9, 4:3, 1:1, 3:4, 9:16","notes":"Official guide: landscape gives far fewer unwanted subtitles than portrait","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"generate_audio","values":"boolean, default true","notes":"Adds SFX, ambience and lip-synced speech; costs the same either way","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"seed","values":"not an input on r2v (output only); text-to-video accepts an integer seed","notes":"Seeds will not lock continuity between two r2v calls","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video","name":"bitrate_mode / codec / end_user_id","values":"standard|high; auto|H264|H265; string","notes":"Optional","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt"},{"model_id":"bytedance/seedance-2.0/image-to-video","name":"image_url / end_image_url","values":"start frame (required) / end frame (optional)","notes":"Last-frame chaining: pass clip 1's final frame as image_url of clip 2. Community calls this weaker than passing the full clip as a video reference","source_url":"https://fal.ai/docs/model-api-reference/video-generation-api/bytedance-seedance-2.0-text-to-video.md"}],"audio":{"generates_audio":"Yes. Seedance 2.0 generates audio jointly with the video in one pass: dialogue/narration with lip-sync, SFX, ambience and music. generate_audio defaults to true on fal at no extra cost. If audio is left unspecified, the model tends to add its own BGM, so ask for silence or 'no music' explicitly.","speech_or_narration":"It can speak a given line. OFFICIAL (BytePlus ModelArk Seedance 2.0 prompt guide): dialogue goes in curly braces {}, e.g. 'One of them smiles and asks {How did the exam go?}'. A less common language must be named ('says in Japanese {...}'). The same guide's own examples also use double quotes: 'The woman speaks first with a playful tone: \"...\"'. fal and Replicate docs say to use double quotes. For VOICE-OVER (off-screen narrator) the official example uses: 'Voiceover: A deep, serene male voice says: '<line>'', optionally followed by '> Text Integration: Render the narration as subtitles at the bottom-center... synchronized with audio timing.' Voice timbre can be locked with an audio reference: 'Use the low, thick, warm ... male voice of @Audio 1 to say ...'. Describing the voice in detail (age, pitch, texture, pace) improves the match. Speaker attribution is done in prose (who says it, in what tone) placed right before the line. For our first-person exhibit narration: either 'Voiceover: <voice description> says: {line}' (off-screen narrator, no lip-sync needed, safest for an inanimate object), or attach the line to the object as speaker. The second is risky: the model may invent a mouth or face. That risk is inferred, not documented.","language_notes":"Official: dialogue language must be consistent within a prompt. Do not mix Chinese and English except for proper nouns. Official: polyphonic or rare characters get mispronounced, so replace them with common homophones. By analogy, unusual proper nouns in English (artist names, Latin/Greek terms) may be mispronounced and are worth respelling phonetically (inferred). Community (heyuan110): write the prompt in the same language as any on-screen text, because mixing languages raises the garble rate. English narration is supported; 2.5 marketing claims 10+ languages for dialogue.","source_urls":["https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","https://fal.ai/docs/model-api-reference/video-generation-api/bytedance-seedance-2.0-text-to-video.md","https://replicate.com/bytedance/seedance-2.0","https://fal.ai/learn/tools/seedance-2-0-prompting-guide","https://www.heyuan110.com/posts/ai/2026-07-11-seedance-2-prompt-guide/","https://seedance2.so/blog/seedance-2-consistent-voice"]},"prompt_findings":[{"finding":"Multi-shot in one generation: use 'Shot 1: / Shot 2: / Shot 3:' blocks in event order. Do NOT impose per-shot durations. The official guide says the model's support for precise timing (e.g. 0-3 s) is unstable, and forcing durations can cause abnormal results. Let the model pace the cuts.","example":"Shot 1: Side shot of a street alley; the man slowly starts running... Shot 2: The man knocks over a fruit stand; the camera shakes quickly and gives a close-up... Shot 3: ...the camera slowly pulls back and freezes on the empty street.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Per-shot content order (official): (1) camera move or transition ('slowly push in from a wide shot', 'fixed camera position', 'cut to...'), (2) subject action/expression, (3) position/space, (4) audio for that shot (SFX, voices, BGM). Transitions between shots are written as 'Cut to a close-up of...', 'The camera cuts back to...', 'Then the camera pans right and cuts to the frame and composition shown in Image 4'.","example":"Shot 2: Girl @Image 1 pushes the door open... The camera cuts to an indoor medium shot... One of them smiles and asks {How did the exam go? Did you pass?}.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Conflicting community practice: Atlabs and Luma recommend timecode headers like [00:00-00:05], which Atlabs says act as hard cuts. heyuan110 recommends 'Seconds 1-5:' timelines for clips over 8 s. These conflict with the official warning that precise timing is unstable. Recommendation for the script agent: emit 'Shot N:' labels and keep target seconds in our own schema, not in the prompt (or at most a soft hint).","example":"[00:00-00:05] wide shot ... [00:05-00:10] close-up ...","source_url":"https://www.atlabs.ai/blog/the-ultimate-seedance-2.0-prompting-guide-47-prompts-2026","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Official special-character table: {} = dialogue, () = music, <> = sound effect, [] = subtitles/on-screen text. Use the same language for all dialogue.","example":"{Hello, world} / (fast-paced rock music is playing in the background) / < dog barking can be heard in the distance > / [Chapter One: Departure]","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Voice-over pattern from the official appendix: 'Voiceover: <voice description> says: '<line>''. Optionally ask for synced subtitles with '> Text Integration: Render the narration as subtitles at the bottom-center...'. If you do NOT want subtitles, add 'Keep it subtitle-free; avoid generating any text or subtitles'. The model adds subtitles unprompted, especially in portrait.","example":"I2V: A time-lapse of a mountain landscape ... Voiceover: A deep, serene male voice says: '<line>'","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Keep spoken lines short and split long speech across shots (one sentence per cut). Long monologues drift out of lip-sync. Heavy dialogue in action-heavy shots degrades both motion and speech. For 15 s, community practice suggests roughly 2-4 short sentences (about 30-40 English words at narration pace; the word count is inferred from typical speech rate of ~2.5 words/s).","example":"Shot 1 ... Voiceover says: {I was carved from a single block of marble.} Shot 2 ... {For two thousand years I have watched you.}","source_url":"https://fal.ai/learn/tools/seedance-2-0-prompting-guide","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Narration end-of-clip artefact (official FAQ): when a video contains narration, abrupt clicks or cut-off noise often appear at the end. Fix by regenerating or applying an audio fade-out in post. Implication: end each 15 s clip's narration ~1 s before the end and fade audio when stitching.","example":"Apply volume envelope fade to 0 over the last frames.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Reference images: refer by number and bind subject to asset every time it is mentioned ('<Subject>@Image 1'), or define a label once ('Define the bronze helmet in Image 1 as the helmet') and reuse exactly that label. Put the most important asset first. Asset IDs cannot replace <Image N>. Assign each reference an explicit role (e.g. '@Image 3 as scene reference').","example":"Use the woman in red from @Image 1 as the female lead, use the cliff and bamboo forest environment in @Image 3 as the scene reference, refer to the overall camera movement in @Video 1, and synchronize the background sound effects with @Audio 1.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Reference syntax variants seen in docs: fal and the official guide use @Image1 / @Image 1. Replicate and fal T2V docs show [Image1]. Atlabs uses @[image1]. The official text also uses plain 'Image 1'. Safest for fal: '@Image1' matching upload order.","example":"The character from [Image1] performs the dance from [Video1].","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Multi-view references: for PRODUCTS/objects the official guide supports 'multi-perspective subject reference' (e.g. three images of the same camera, rotated 360 degrees). For CHARACTERS it warns that multi-view or three-view sheets get read as different subjects and cause duplicates ('twins'). For a museum exhibit, front/side/detail views are fine, but bind them explicitly to ONE subject: 'Define the vase in Image 1, Image 2 and Image 3 as the vase'. Also add an anti-duplicate constraint.","example":"Use the cameras featured in Image 1, Image 2 and Image 3 ... slowly rotates 360° ... clearly displaying the front, sides and back of each camera.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Anti-duplicate constraint (official), useful when several views of one exhibit are supplied: append a global line forbidding identical copies of the subject in one frame.","example":"Throughout the video, ... Do not generate duplicate avatars or a twin effect. Keep only a single corresponding [object] in the same frame.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Recommended asset count: 4-5 total (1-2 subject images + 1 scene image + 1 camera video + 1 audio). Do not max out the 9/3/3 limits, because priority confusion causes style conflicts and subject blur.","example":"@Image1 front view, @Image2 detail, @Image3 gallery scene, @Audio1 narrator timbre","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Advanced prompt formula (official): precise subject + action details + scene/environment + lighting & colour tone + camera movement + visual style + image quality + constraints. Lock who/what first, then where/atmosphere, then how to shoot, then style/quality/constraints.","example":"...The entire video should have a high-definition cinematic documentary style, with warm tones and soft lighting.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Prompt length: there is no official recommended length (third-party report: ByteDance said 'no'). Official FAQ: do not paste the whole script as the prompt; redundant copy confuses the model. Community advice ranges from 60-100 words (single shot; heyuan110 says adherence drops with length and the first 2-3 instructions are reliably followed) to 200-500 words for multi-shot timecoded prompts (Atlabs). Volcengine cap reportedly about 1000 English words. Practical target for a 3-4-shot 15 s clip: ~120-250 words, essentials front-loaded (inferred).","example":"Keep the subject binding and the narration lines in the first half of the prompt.","source_url":"https://www.heyuan110.com/posts/ai/2026-07-11-seedance-2-prompt-guide/","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Camera vocabulary: standard terms work well (medium shot, close-up, wide shot, slow push-in, smooth lateral tracking, fixed shot). Use ONLY ONE camera movement per shot. Stacking push/pull/pan increases instability. Community-tested additions: dolly, pan, tilt, crane, orbit/arc, rack focus, locked-off, macro, low front angle, long lens, drone overhead, 'one continuous take, no cuts'. For static objects, slow push-in, slow orbit, macro detail pass and rack focus suit (inferred).","example":"Shot 1: Fixed camera, wide shot of the gallery. Shot 2: Slow push-in to a macro close-up of the inscription.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Motion: prefer slow, gentle, continuous small movements. Avoid high-burst actions (sprints, jumps, rolls). Specify the body part, range, speed and force. Express emotion through concrete physical details, not abstract words. For an object narrator: light, dust motes, camera motion and slow environmental change, rather than making the object move (inferred).","example":"slowly raise a hand, slightly lower the head; 'letting out a long breath, tense shoulders completely relaxing' instead of 'relief'","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Constraint words to always append (official): 'keep it subtitle-free / avoid generating any text or subtitles' (unless subtitles are wanted), 'do not generate a logo', 'do not generate a watermark', plus an explicit style word to prevent style drift. Avoid vague quality words ('epic', '8k', 'masterpiece'); fal says they render nothing useful.","example":"Keep it subtitle-free. Do not generate watermarks. Do not generate logos. Photorealistic museum documentary style.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Text artefacts: on-screen text formula is [Text content] + [Timing] + [Position] + [Entrance style], [Colour/font]. Use common characters, no special symbols. Unrequested subtitles cannot be fully prevented: use negative constraints, text-free reference images, and landscape aspect ratio. Exact logos and wordmarks should come from an image reference or post-production. For museum labels or dates on screen, overlay in post rather than generating them (inferred).","example":"Display subtitles at the bottom-center with the text. The subtitles must be perfectly synchronized with the audio rhythm and pacing.","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Audio must be specified or the model adds BGM. Name diegetic sounds (footsteps, room tone, distant murmur) and state 'no music' if wanted. fal treats the audio part as a 'sound brief'.","example":"Audio: quiet gallery room tone, distant footsteps on marble, (soft solo cello under the narration), no other music.","source_url":"https://fal.ai/learn/tools/seedance-2-0-prompting-guide","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Failure modes, official FAQ: face/ID drift (use a clean headshot and put it first), unexpected subtitles, platform logo/watermark, style drift to live-action, jump cuts at extension joins, duplicated subjects from multi-view refs, quality degradation over repeated extensions (colour blotches), special effects not matching text (use a reference video), more than 4 reference people unstable, narration end-of-clip noise, mispronunciation, voice reference mismatch.","example":"see FAQ sections","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Failure modes, community: instruction overflow (later instructions ignored), naked references bleeding lighting/framing (assign roles), stacked camera moves causing jitter, scene cramming (too many locations in a short clip causes smeared morphing), maxed references making subjects look cardboard, long monologues losing lip-sync.","example":"Match the number of locations to duration: 1 location per 15 s clip is safest.","source_url":"https://www.heyuan110.com/posts/ai/2026-07-11-seedance-2-prompt-guide/","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Voice consistency across two generations: pass the same voice sample as @Audio1 in both calls and describe the voice identically in text (official tip: detailed voice-characteristic description greatly improves matching; keep the lines' tone close to the reference). Community: the result is recognisably similar, not identical. For exact voice, record or TTS the narration externally and use Seedance for visuals (fal's own 2.5 test used Seed Audio 1.0 for VO). Practical option: take clip 1's narration audio (or a TTS sample) as @Audio1 for clip 2.","example":"Use the low, thick, warm, and finely grainy middle-aged male voice of @Audio 1 to say {...}","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"Same-narrator caveat: community reports voice consistency via @Audio is approximate. For exact replication they recommend external voice recording plus Seedance visuals only.","example":"generate_audio=true for SFX/ambience, VO mixed in post","source_url":"https://seedance2.so/blog/seedance-2-consistent-voice","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Visual continuity across two 15 s clips, official options: (a) video extension, giving clip 1 as Video 1 and prompting 'Extend Video 1 backward/forward...' or 'Generate the content after Video 1: ...' (the model extracts transition frames; do NOT write 'reference Video 1' or it becomes a reference task). (b) Segmented stitching: generate independent clips and cut on a scene change. The official advice is to end clip 1 on a transition cut and start clip 2 on a new scene, which hides joins. Extension joins can jump or roll back; fix by trimming 6 frames from the end of the earlier clip and 1 frame from the start of the next. Repeated extension degrades quality.","example":"Generate the content after Video 1: the camera slowly orbits to the back of the vase while the narrator continues {...}","source_url":"https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/modelark/seedance-2-0-prompt-guide","confidence":"official"},{"finding":"fal constraint for extension: video_urls combined length is 2-15 s and resolution about 480p-720p. A full 15 s clip 1 at 1080p cannot be passed as-is: downscale it to 720p and possibly trim to its last few seconds (inferred from limits).","example":"Pass the last 5 s of clip 1 at 720p as @Video1.","source_url":"https://fal.ai/models/bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video/llms.txt","confidence":"inferred"},{"finding":"Continuity via restated anchors: reuse identical reference images in the same order in both calls. Use the same subject label, style line, lighting line and constraint block verbatim. Start the second prompt with 'Continue from the previous stage' and restate fixed facts, using a [Maintain Consistency] block. Community also reports last-frame-only chaining (I2V image_url) as weaker than passing the full prior clip.","example":"[Maintain Consistency] same bronze helmet @Image1, same dim gallery with warm spotlight, same slow documentary pacing.","source_url":"https://kylon.io/blog/seedance-2-5-prompt-guide-2026","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Image-first prompts: do not re-describe what the reference image already shows. Spend prompt words on motion, camera and sound. Use text for semantics and spatial decisions, references for identity.","example":"Instead of describing the vase's pattern, write 'the vase from @Image1'.","source_url":"https://www.atlabs.ai/blog/the-ultimate-seedance-2.0-prompting-guide-47-prompts-2026","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Iterate cheaply: test at ~5 s or 480p to lock the look, then render full length. Change one variable per retry.","example":"duration \"5\", resolution \"480p\" for drafts","source_url":"https://replicate.com/bytedance/seedance-2.0","confidence":"community"},{"finding":"Seedance 2.5 on fal does 4-30 s natively in one call, with the same @Image grammar. This changes the s3/s4 script structure.","Narration audio: let Seedance voice it (native, approximate voice match across two clips via @Audio1) or generate VO separately (TTS) and mix in post with generate_audio for SFX/ambience only? With the latter, the prompt should say 'no speech' to avoid double voices.","Delimiters: the official guide documents {} for dialogue, () for music, <> for SFX, [] for subtitles, but its own examples also use double quotes and the fal docs use quotes. Do we standardise on {} or quotes? Needs a test render.","Should prompts include target seconds per shot? Official says precise timing is unstable; community (Atlabs/Luma) uses [00:00-00:05] headers. Proposed: Shot N labels only, with seconds kept in our schema for checks.","Multi-view refs for objects: the official guide supports multi-perspective product references but warns multi-view character sheets cause duplicates. Confirm whether passing a trimmed tail of clip 1 as @Video1 gives a smooth join, or whether a hard cut on a scene change (official recommendation) is enough.","Museum objects with real faces (portraits, busts): the official FAQ notes celebrity-resemblance blocking on face drift.   You are judging two candidate scripts for a 30-second film, narrated in the first person by a museum exhibit (Hope, the blue whale skeleton in the Natural History Museum, London), shown on a visitor's phone right after they photograph it. Judge only what is written below; do not open any files.
  
  Sourced facts available to both writers (anything else stated as fact is an invention): Hope was a juvenile female blue whale; she stranded on a sandbar at the mouth of Wexford Harbour, Ireland, on the morning of 25 March 1891. The 4.5-tonne skeleton was sold to the Natural History Museum for £250. It hung in the Mammal Hall from 1934; moved to Hintze Hall and unveiled in July 2017, replacing the Dippy the Diplodocus cast (there 1979 to January 2017). Named Hope "as a symbol of humanity's power to shape a sustainable future". The skeleton is 25.2 m long. (Unsourced, must not be stated: 221 bones, diving-lunge pose.) The visitor photographed the skeleton from the first-floor balcony of Hintze Hall, looking across at it hanging mid-dive above a crowd.
  
  Criteria (from the team's quality bar): hook in the first 2-3 s; answers what a visitor standing there wonders; one idea; counterpoint (lines add what the picture cannot show); truth (no claim beyond the facts, no added qualifiers like died/alone/finished growing); renderable by a video model (one camera move and action per shot, no text on screen, no two subject states in one shot); spoken quality and timing; ending that lands and sends the eye back to the real object.
  
  Read both scripts in full, then compare criterion by criterion and choose overall. Be concrete; quote lines.
  
  === SCRIPT A ===
  TITLE: Not Even Grown
  LOGLINE: Hope turns the visitor's awe at her size into a surprise: she was only a juvenile, cut short on an Irish sandbar in 1891, and the museum named her Hope as a promise about the future.
  LENGTH: 30s
  
  --- Segment 1 (15.0s) ---
  [0.0-5.0s] Wide, low angle from the hall floor / Slow upward tilt. The camera tilts up from tiny visitors on the stone floor along the endless chain of vertebrae of the suspended blue whale skeleton. Hope, the pale ivory-cream blue whale skeleton, hung mid-dive from steel cables under a high Romanesque ceiling; terracotta arches, soft daylight, small figures of visitors gazing up
  [5.0-9.5s] Medium, directly beneath the body / Steady forward dolly looking straight up. Long curved ribs of the blue whale skeleton slide past overhead one after another as the camera glides beneath them. Rows of pale ivory-cream ribs and vertebrae against the terracotta arches and high ceiling, warm soft light catching the bone texture
  [9.5-15.0s] Wide underwater / Slow drift alongside, then gentle tilt up. A lone, slender young blue whale, small in the frame, glides through empty grey-green water and rises toward pale grey surface light. Deep blue-grey mottled skin and pale ribbed throat, wide empty grey-green water, soft shafts of grey light from the surface; frame ends on the brightening surface
    VO @1.2s: "Twenty-five metres of me."
    VO @3.4s: "And I was a girl, not finished growing."
    VO @6.4s: "Four and a half tonnes, just in bone."
    VO @10.6s: "Out there, I was blue, not bone."
    SOUND: Hushed, cavernous museum hall: soft reverberant murmur of visitors and distant footsteps on stone, dissolving into muffled deep-water hum; Faint creak of suspension cables as the camera rises; Soft airy whoosh as ribs pass overhead; Underwater rush and a low, distant blue whale call as the scene turns to water; music: Sparse, slow piano notes with a quiet sustained string pad, growing a little fuller as the water appears
  --- Segment 2 (15.0s) ---
  [15.0-19.0s] Aerial, wide / Slow descending crane down through grey sky. The camera sinks from pale grey sky toward the mouth of a harbour where a long sandbar lies in shallow water. Grey morning light over Wexford Harbour, Ireland; flat silver water, a pale strip of wet sand, low green shoreline in mist
  [19.0-22.5s] Low, close to the sand / Static, ground level. A thin sheet of tide water washes up the wet sand and around the still blue-grey flank of the young whale. Glistening wet sand, shallow grey water, the deep blue-grey mottled side of the slender young blue whale lying motionless, soft grey morning light
  [22.5-26.0s] Medium to wide, low angle in the hall / Upward crane from visitors' faces to the ceiling. The camera rises past upturned visitors' faces to reveal the blue whale skeleton plunging head-down in its mid-dive pose. Hope, the pale ivory-cream blue whale skeleton, suspended mid-dive under terracotta arches, visitors small below, warm daylight
  [26.0-30.0s] Low-angle close-up / Slow push in. Coloured light from the stained glass slides across the skull and long lower jawbones of the blue whale skeleton. Patches of red, blue and amber light moving over pale ivory-cream bone texture, terracotta arches soft in the background
    VO @16.0s: "Twenty-fifth of March, 1891."
    VO @19.4s: "Morning, on a sandbar in Wexford Harbour."
    VO @22.6s: "Look up. I'm still diving."
    VO @26.0s: "They named me Hope — for a future you can still shape."
    SOUND: Cold coastal morning: light wind, gentle lapping of shallow water, distant gulls; changing to the warm, reverberant hush of the museum hall; Soft wind gust as the camera descends; Thin wash of tide water hissing over wet sand; Echoing footsteps and a quiet intake of breath from a visitor as the camera rises; Low, resonant whale call fading under the final line; music: A single low cello line over the harbour, then warm strings lift gently at the return to the hall and resolve softly on the name
  
  === SCRIPT B ===
  TITLE: Young Giant
  LOGLINE: The skeleton notices the visitor stepping back to fit it into one photo, gives its length, then reveals it was a young whale.
  LENGTH: 30s
  
  --- Segment 1 (15.0s) ---
  [0.0-6.0s] wide, from first-floor balcony / slow pull-back. Camera retreats through a terracotta arch until the whole skeleton fits the frame. Terracotta arch edges slide in until the whole skeleton fits; tiny visitors below.
  [6.0-11.0s] medium, along the bones / slow drift left to right. Camera glides from the jaw tips along the spine toward the tail. From jaw tips along the spine toward the tail.
  [11.0-15.0s] low angle looking up / static. Dust drifts slowly through skylight beams past the ribs. Ribs against the bright glass roof; dust in skylight beams.
    VO @1.2s: "You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in."
    VO @8.0s: "From jaw to tail, I measure just over twenty-five metres."
    SOUND: Large stone hall: soft echoing crowd murmur, distant footsteps on stone; a child's distant gasp echoing below; music: Sparse solo cello with soft piano, slow and warm; ends on one held cello note that dies away into near silence before the cut.
  --- Segment 2 (15.0s) ---
  [15.0-19.5s] wide, looking up from below / static. The living whale glides slowly overhead beneath the bright rippling surface. The whale glides overhead under the sunlit rippling surface.
  [19.5-24.5s] medium close, alongside the whale / slow drift alongside. The whale swims forward, long flipper slowly sweeping. Long flipper sweeps through blue water beside the mottled flank.
  [24.5-30.0s] wide, elevated side view from first-floor balcony, matching the visitor's photo / slow push-in. Visitors walk slowly across the floor beneath the skeleton. Visitors walk beneath the skeleton in soft skylight.
    VO @16.0s: "That number isn't the surprising part."
    VO @19.0s: "Out in the sea, I was a juvenile. Just a girl."
    VO @24.8s: "Look up again. All of this — and I was young."
    SOUND: Underwater hush with soft low rumble, then the echoing murmur of the stone hall; muffled whoosh of water as the whale passes overhead; music: Sparse solo cello with soft piano, slow and warm; opens fresh on a single soft piano note and resolves on a quiet held cello chord at the end.
  
  Judge as a museum interpretation specialist who is strict about factual integrity.

One more surprising fact is a reveal, not a turn. At 15 seconds any single idea will do, and the lead through-line is a suggestion, not a requirement. Never justify a length by how few facts a concept needs; prefer focus over coverage within the length you are given.\" (3) Structural option, to try as its own A/B after (2): add `duration_s: Literal[15, 30]` and `through_line: int` to schemas.Concept. Sophie and Hope get 30 s only when the lead line is the journey (Wyoming hilltop → 70 donors → London, named for Sophie Herrmann; Wexford sandbar 1891 → £250 → Mammal Hall → Dippy's place, named Hope). That journey is then what S2 must film. The filler lines and fact-splitting disappear because the length matches the story actually told."},{"title":"Exact replacement text for prompts/s1_digest.md","severity":"high","evidence":"Current prompt: 'Choose 30 only if the facts support a real arc: a beginning, a turn and an end.' Nothing defines a turn, nothing ties length to a single story, and 'be accurate and generous with material' plus 'List every usable fact' make the pack's size the model's proxy for richness. The schema field `richness` (thin/medium/rich) appears nowhere in the prompt. It is decorative and uncorrelated with fact count: hope gets 'rich' from 11 facts, sequoia 'medium' from 16, sophie 'rich' from 33.","root_cause":"The prompt's length rule asks about the pack ('the facts support an arc') instead of about one story. It gives no operational test, and the undefined 'richness' label invites a judgement by volume.","change":"Replace the whole file with:\n\nYou prepare the raw material for a very short film about one museum exhibit. A screenwriter will work only from what you hand over, so be accurate, never invent, and decide how long the film should be.\n\nYou get the exhibit's knowledge pack (each section ends with its source) and, when there is one, the visitor's photo.\n\nFacts. List every usable fact as a short plain statement with its source URL copied exactly from the pack. Give each separate claim its own fact so that every spoken line can cite exactly what supports it. Keep numbers, dates, tense and quoted words exactly as the pack has them; do not round, convert or combine them, and never work out new figures (durations between two dates, totals, ages). Leave out anything marked UNSOURCED, anything the pack says may no longer be true, and the appearance notes: those are for pictures, not for narration. Mark each fact about: \"exhibit\" when it is about this object's own story (what it was, its size, where it came from, what happened to it, why it is here), or \"context\" when it is general background (how a process works in general, world statistics, the species in general). How many facts there are says nothing about how long the film should be.\n\nConflicts. When the pack reports two versions of something (two counts, two dates), list the conflict and say which wording is safe. Also list anything a writer could easily confuse (a date that belongs to one event but not another).\n\nNo invention anywhere. The same rule covers everything else you write: through-lines, motifs, questions, the emotional core and the length reason. You may combine and contrast facts, but not add events, durations, causes, intentions, feelings or qualities the pack does not contain. Do not say an animal suffered, died alone, was lost, was the first, last or only, was found by chance, or was renamed, unless a fact says so, and do not give a person an age the pack does not give. When you interpret, put the interpretation in its own sentence beginning \"This suggests\" and keep every factual sentence exact. Leave out general knowledge about the species or period, even if true.\n\nVisitor questions. List the three to five questions a person standing in front of this object most likely has (is it real? how big was it? how did it get here? what was it like alive or in use? why is it called that?). If there is a photo, put first the question its framing suggests. Ask what a visitor would wonder, not what the pack happens to answer. For each, give the ids of the facts that answer it, or none if the pack cannot answer it.\n\nThrough-lines. Find one to three stories this object could tell about itself in one film. Each has a beginning, a turn and an end, each resting on exhibit facts (context facts may colour a story, but never carry its turn). A turn is a change in what the object is or means: from animal to specimen, from buried to displayed, from hidden to seen, from there to here. A list of dates is not a turn, and neither is one more surprising fact. For each through-line, write its spine: the fewest plain spoken sentences that tell the whole story, using only listed facts, with numbers written in words. Then give the length it needs. It is 15 if the spine fits in about 28 spoken words, or if there is no real turn. It is 30 only if the spine needs more than that because the turn carries the story into a second time or place that can fill its own 15-second segment.\n\nVisual motifs. Concrete things a camera could show: places, objects, light, movement, textures, including what the visitor can see in front of them right now. Never text, numbers, prices, labels or documents on screen.\n\nEmotional core. One sentence: the single thing about this exhibit most likely to move a visitor standing in front of it.\n\nLength. Choose the lead through-line: the one that best answers the visitor's first question and is most likely to move them. The film's length is the length that through-line needs, never the length the pack could fill. A big pack often makes a 15-second film. A small pack makes a 30-second one only if its best story truly has two halves. When unsure, choose 15. In one or two sentences, explain the choice by the lead through-line's spine, not by how much material there is.\n\nSchema (schemas.py): add `about: Literal[\"exhibit\",\"context\"]` to Fact. Replace `visitor_questions: list[str]` with `list[VisitorQuestion]`, where VisitorQuestion has `question: str` and `answer_fact_ids: list[str]`. Add a ThroughLine model with `name`, `beginning`, `turn` and `end` (str); `turn_fact_ids` and `fact_ids` (list[str]); `spine` (str, described as 'fewest spoken sentences telling the whole story, numbers in words, listed facts only'); and `needs_s: Literal[15,30]`. Replace `richness` with `through_lines: list[ThroughLine]` (1-3) and `lead_line: int`. Keep `duration_s` and `duration_reason`. Update run.py line 176 (prints sh.richness) and the report's visitor-question/story-material sections to the new fields. Archive the current file as prompts/archive/v1.2/s1_digest.md.","expected_effect":"The length decision becomes checkable and story-based. Sequoia's best spine is about 22 words with no exhibit-level turn beyond the reveal, so the film is 15 s. Hope's and Sophie's journey spines run about 45-55 words with a then/there to now/here turn that maps onto the two segments and the hard cut, so they get 30 s, which matches expect.json. Fact atomicity is kept for audit, while volume no longer reads as richness."},{"title":"Add code checks that enforce the length rule and catch padding (S1, S2, S3)","severity":"high","evidence":"checks.material only verifies sources, digits and the expect.json duration, and its output is printed but not acted on ('s1: length chosen 30s; regression expectation for this exhibit is 15s' sat in the sequoia report while the run continued). Padding was invisible to every check: sequoia lines 4-5 both cite [rings_revealed], and hope seg 2 lines 2-3 both cite [juvenile_female]. Hope's S1 visual_motifs include 'A price of £250 written as on an old ledger' (text on screen), and no check covers motifs.","root_cause":"The harness has no consistency link between the S1 length, the chosen concept and the script's fact use. The text-on-screen regex is applied to concepts and assets but not to S1 motifs. S1 problems never trigger a retry.","change":"checks.material: (a) lead = sheet.through_lines[sheet.lead_line]. Add a problem if sheet.duration_s != lead.needs_s. (b) n = spoken_words(lead.spine). Add a problem if lead.needs_s == 30 and n <= 28 ('lead spine is n spoken words: a 15 s story'), and if lead.needs_s == 15 and n > 32. (c) Add a problem if needs_s == 30 and every id in lead.turn_fact_ids is an about=='context' fact, or turn_fact_ids is empty. (d) Add a problem for unknown ids in through_lines, visitor_questions or story fields. (e) Add a problem when _text_on_screen(motif) is true for any visual_motif. In run.py, if material() returns problems other than the expect.json one, rerun S1 once with them as feedback (never feed the expect.json value back: it is the regression oracle). checks.concepts: if sheet.duration_s == 30, add a problem when the chosen concept's fact_ids miss every id in the lead line's turn_fact_ids ('30 s film but the chosen concept drops the lead turn: it is a 15 s story'). checks.script: add a problem when two consecutive narration lines in a segment have the same non-empty fact_ids set ('two lines carry one fact: padding'), and add a problem when sc.duration_s == 30 and total spoken words < 40.","expected_effect":"Each check fires on these runs. Sequoia trips (b) or (c) at S1. Hope v1.2 trips the concept check ([length, juvenile_female] does not include the journey turn) and the consecutive-fact check. Sequoia's attempt 2 trips the consecutive-fact check, and attempt 0 (23 words) trips the 40-word floor. Hope's ledger motif is flagged before S2 can use it. A length mismatch is caught at S1 or S2, where fixing it costs one cheap call, instead of surviving three S3/S4 rounds."},{"title":"S1 truthfulness: facts are clean, but S1's free-text fields add small inventions; the 'this suggests' marker is used as a blanket prefix","severity":"medium","evidence":"The fact lists are correct in all three runs: UNSOURCED items excluded (221 bones, lunge, diameter, 80-90%), numbers exact, 360 dropped from sophie digital_model, 'only Stegosaurus outside USA' excluded, the sequoia 1893/1891 conflict reported plus a good extra note that 1893 is the arrival year only, sophie 'over 300' given as the safe wording, and hope's 'do not say renamed' guard. Inventions appear in the other fields. Sequoia duration_reason: 'sits under dust for over a century' (the pack never says how long the dust lay; this conflates display_century with dust_inch). Sequoia story_material: 'outlasting a century of change outside' (unmarked) and 'kept for keepers not yet born' (adds intent). Sophie story_material: 'buried on a hilltop in Wyoming for 150 million years, she was found by chance' (neither the duration of burial nor 'by chance' is in the pack). Sophie emotional_core: 'This suggests the most moving thing: an animal that lay almost whole ... for 150 million years was found by chance ... carrying the name of a girl, Sophie' (the pack says 'daughter', with no age; one 'This suggests' prefix covers several unmarked claims). Hope: 'a single lost animal' ('lost' is a quality) and 'Hope is a skeleton of an actual whale' (an inference). There are minor tense rewrites: 'The skeleton weighs 4.5 tonnes' for 'The 4.5-tonne skeleton was sold', and '15 billion trees are being cut down every year'. Downstream, S3 rounded 25.2 m to 'just over twenty-five metres' and wrote 'Just a girl' / 'I was a juvenile when I swam', echoing the same anthropomorphising drift.","root_cause":"The prompt's no-invention list covers facts and 'story material' but names neither durations, chance and intent, nor the length reason. 'Mark it as yours' lets one marker at the front launder a whole sentence. 'Keep numbers exactly' does not mention tense.","change":"These are covered by the replacement prompt's 'No invention anywhere' paragraph: it extends the rule to the through-lines, questions, emotional core and length reason; adds 'durations, causes, intentions', 'was lost', 'was found by chance' and 'do not give a person an age the pack does not give'; requires each interpretation in its own sentence starting 'This suggests' with factual sentences kept exact; and adds 'tense' to the exact-wording rule. Optionally add an S4 instruction or check: flag a spoken number that is a rounded form of a cited fact number ('twenty-five' vs 25.2) unless the fact itself says 'about'.","expected_effect":"S2/S3 stop inheriting quiet S1 embellishments ('by chance', 'for 150 million years', 'a girl', 'under dust for a century') that the reviewer must otherwise catch line by line. The exact-number guarantee S1 already meets carries through to the spoken numbers."},{"title":"Visitor questions are good, but they mix in pack-led questions and do not say which ones the pack can answer","severity":"medium","evidence":"Hope (with photo) is good: the first question comes from the framing ('Just how big is this whale? (The wide shot with tiny people beneath...)'), and 'What used to hang here before her?' is a real question the pack answers (Dippy). Sophie is good ('Is this a real skeleton, and how much of it is actually her?'). Sequoia (no photo) includes 'Why does the wood look so clean and glossy?', which follows the pack's conservation section rather than a likely first-time question, yet S2 built the chosen film on it ('Answers: Why does the wood look so clean and glossy?'). Sequoia's 'how did it end up in the museum?' cannot be answered from the pack (only 'since 1893'), and nothing marks it as unanswerable, so S2 could promise an answer it cannot source.","root_cause":"The schema has questions as bare strings. The prompt does not separate 'what a visitor wonders' from 'what the pack covers', and it does not ask for answerability.","change":"Use the VisitorQuestion {question, answer_fact_ids} schema and the replacement wording 'Ask what a visitor would wonder, not what the pack happens to answer. For each, give the ids of the facts that answer it, or none if the pack cannot answer it.' Add one line to S2: 'Answer a question whose answer_fact_ids are not empty; prefer the first one.' Add to checks.concepts: a problem if the chosen concept's answers_question matches a question with empty answer_fact_ids (fuzzy match on text, or add an `answers_question_index: int` to Concept).","expected_effect":"S2 targets questions the pack can actually answer, starting with the visitor's real first question. For sequoia that is age and rings, which supports the 15 s 'You're counting my rings' or the dust-to-rings film told at 15 s. Unanswerable questions no longer pull concepts toward invention."},{"title":"S4 length_fit has no anchor for padding, so a 15 s story at 30 s scores 4","severity":"medium","evidence":"length_fit = 4 on all three v1.2 chosen scripts. The sequoia reviewer saw the padding and kept it: 'two short lines carrying one fact... Keep both lines, since they are true and give the breathing room'. In hope, 'That number isn't the surprising part.' and the repeated 'young' ending went unflagged in v1, v1.1 and v1.2.","root_cause":"prompts/s4_review.md line 20, 'length_fit: the narration breathes, and the chosen length suits the amount of story', rewards breathing room and never names padding.","change":"In prompts/s4_review.md replace that line with: \"length_fit: 5 = every line adds something the previous one did not and the film needs every second; 3 = one line only restates, delays or splits a fact ('That number isn't the surprising part', one fact spread over two lines), or a shot exists only to fill time; 2 or lower = the story could be told in one 15-second segment, so the film should be 15 seconds (say so as a blocking issue at the material stage, not a rewrite).\" Because a 30→15 change cannot be fixed by S3, run.py should treat an S4 issue tagged where='length' as needing S1/S2 to rerun at 15 s, or as NEEDS_HUMAN with that reason.","expected_effect":"The reviewer stops scoring stretched films 4 and reports length mismatches as a material-stage fault. This also gives the S1/S2 changes a second, independent signal in A/B comparisons."},{"title":"Regression expectations for hope/sophie assume the journey is the lead story; make that explicit so the new rule does not 'fail' a valid 15 s choice","severity":"low","evidence":"Under the new rule the size-reveal through-line for Hope ('twenty-five point two metres, and I was a juvenile', about 12 words) and the completeness one for Sophie ('Almost Whole', about 27 words) are 15 s stories. Hope's photo-led first question (size) might make S1 pick the size line as lead and choose 15, which contradicts expect.json duration_s 30.","root_cause":"expect.json fixes a length per exhibit, not a length per lead story. The METHOD table says 'rich → 30 s', which encodes the old fact-volume rule.","change":"Keep expect.json duration_s, but document in METHOD.md section 3 and the expect 'why' that hope and sophie are 30 s because their strongest story is the journey (sandbar → hall / hilltop → London). Add an optional expect field `lead_line_hint` ('journey') that checks.material compares by fact ids: for hope the lead fact_ids must include stranding_date and hintze_unveiling or replaced_dippy, and for sophie found_2003 and arrived_2013 or named_sophie. In the S1 prompt's Length paragraph, the tie-break 'best answers the visitor's first question and is most likely to move them' should be kept as written. Do not weight toward 30.","expected_effect":"Regression failures will tell you whether S1 chose the wrong story or chose the right story at the wrong length. Those are different bugs."}],"keep":["S1's fact-level accuracy rules: copy source URLs exactly, keep numbers and dates as written, no derived figures (no '38 years' appeared in any Hope run), leave out UNSOURCED, may-no-longer-be-true and appearance notes.
