Only stop treating fact count as richness.","The conflicts section, including the extra 'easily confused' notes the model added on its own (sequoia: 1893 is the arrival year, not the felling year; no gap may be stated; no slice size). Also the safe-wording guidance (sophie 'over 300', hope 'named, not renamed').","The explicit banned-claims list (suffered, died alone, first/last/only, renamed) and 'leave out general knowledge'. Extend it rather than rewrite it.","When there is a photo, the photo-led first visitor question (Hope v1.2: size, from the wide framing).","Sending the photo to S1 and the visual_motifs field, which S1b and S2 use well (the dust/clean before-after and the visitor's own viewpoint).","checks.material's source and digit checks, and the expect.json duration regression check. Keep the expect value out of any feedback sent to the model.","One change at a time. Ship the S1 prompt with its schema and checks as one A/B (s1 variant), then the S2 wording, then the concept-owned duration as a separate step, comparing with --sample 2 on all three exhibits."]},{"lens":"craft","findings":[{"title":"Narration captions the picture: almost every line is a 'fact' read over an image of that same fact, so counterpoint is near zero","severity":"high","evidence":"Across the three chosen scripts, 15 lines are 13 fact, 1 visible and 1 voice (report.md lines_by_kind: hope 3/1/1, sophie 4/0/0, sequoia 6/0/0). Sequoia seg1: 'Under this shine, there was once about an inch of dust' over 'top edge under thick matte grey dust; a gloved hand pulls a soft brush'; seg2 'That work revealed my many rings' over 'tightly packed glossy rings'. Sophie seg1: 'Most Stegosaurus skeletons are found with parts missing' over 'a few separate dark fossil bones lying far apart'; 'Mine lay almost fully joined' over 'A long row of joined dark vertebrae'; seg2 'My plates and tail spikes...' over 'Thin plates' / 'Tail spikes'. Hope: 'From jaw to tail, I measure just over twenty-five metres' over 'Camera glides from the jaw tips along the spine toward the tail'. S4 still scored counterpoint 4 on all three. The narrators were cast as 'gently amused' / 'faintly amused' but no line expresses it.","root_cause":"Prompt (s3_script.md): shots are planned to illustrate each line; the counterpoint rule is stated as an abstract principle ('Picture and words must not say the same thing') with a mute test that a literal illustration passes (the number is not visible, so the picture 'loses' nothing). There is no requirement for any voice line or for a specific image/line collision. Reviewer strictness (s4_review.md counterpoint anchor too loose) lets it through.","change":"prompts/s3_script.md, replace the paragraph starting 'Narration. English, first person...' through '...rewrite or cut it.' with:\n\"Narration. English, first person, spoken by the exhibit. Write the picture and the words as two separate tracks that meet only at the key moment. Rule of thumb: the shot under a line must NOT show the noun the line is about. If the line says dust, show the clean gloss; if the line says pieces, show her whole; if the line gives a length, show the people under it, not the bones being measured. Plan, for each segment, one collision: a single image that changes the meaning of the line over it (youth spoken over the full adult-sized skeleton, the sea spoken over bare bone, a century of dust spoken over a mirror-clean face). Name it in the shot's purpose. Test every line two ways: muted, does the picture still tell its half? Eyes closed, does the line still tell its half? If either half is a duplicate, change the shot, not only the line. At least one line per segment is kind voice and carries the narrator's attitude (a reaction, a confession, a joke at its own expense), with no claims; no voice line may be a line that only announces what comes next ('That number isn't the surprising part').\"\nAlso prompts/s4_review.md: change the counterpoint anchor to 'counterpoint: 5 only if no line names what its own shot shows and at least one image changes how its line is heard; a film where the picture illustrates each line is 2.'","expected_effect":"Shots stop being illustrations; each film gains one memorable image/line collision and at least two lines of personality, which is where 'spoken' and 'hook' scores currently cap at 4. Reviewer will start blocking literal captioning instead of passing it at 4."},{"title":"Shot 'action' and 'visual' duplicate each other or degrade to stubs; this alone pushes 4 of 6 requests over the 250-word cap","severity":"high","evidence":"Hope seg1 rendered: 'Camera glides from the jaw tips along the spine toward the tail. From jaw tips along the spine toward the tail.'; seg2 'The living whale glides slowly overhead beneath the bright rippling surface. The whale glides overhead under the sunlit rippling surface.' Sequoia seg2 actions are stubs 'Light glides.' 'Light settles.' 'Daylight catches the rings.' Sophie seg1 actions are lower-case fragments ('fine dust drifts in the wind.'). Every run's final blocking issue is the word check (hope 293/355, sophie 254, sequoia 291); every S4 rewrite says 'merge each shot's action and visual into one clause'. Three revisions never fixed it because the writer re-fills both fields each time.","root_cause":"Schema/renderer + prompt: schemas.py has both action ('The one main motion') and visual ('What is on screen'), seedance.py line 73 concatenates '{action}. {visual}.' and s3_script.md asks for 'one main action, described as motion' and also a visual 'concretely and briefly', without saying the two are joined verbatim. The writer never sees the 250-word budget, only the reviewer does.","change":"Best fix is harness (render one sentence, or drop 'action' when its words overlap 'visual'), but in prompts/s3_script.md replace 'Describe what is on screen concretely and briefly (at most 25 words per shot visual): place, light, colour, texture, ...' with:\n\"The request builder prints each shot as: framing, camera. action. visual. — one after the other, so never repeat a word between them. action = the single thing that moves, as subject + verb + direction, 4 to 8 words ('dust drifts down through a skylight beam'). visual = the still contents of the frame, no verbs of motion, at most 15 words ('ribs against the bright glass roof, tiny visitors far below'). Never restate the camera move in either field. Budget: each segment's whole request must stay under 230 words, and the voice description, references and constraint line already take about 110, so the three shots together get about 90 words.\"","expected_effect":"Requests drop by roughly 40-70 words each (to ~180-220), removing the single blocking issue that left all three v1.2 runs at NEEDS_HUMAN; revision rounds can then be spent on craft instead of word trimming."},{"title":"Renderer pastes the full subject lock twice when a detail reference is listed before exhibit_now","severity":"high","evidence":"Sequoia seg2 request: '@Image1 is a close detail (a close detail of the slice's rings) of A large round cross-section of giant sequoia trunk ... bark; @Image2 is A large round cross-section of giant sequoia trunk ... bark' (~60 words duplicated, request 291 words). ref_assets order was ['detail_rings','exhibit_now','hintze_balcony'].","root_cause":"Harness code, seedance.py render(): the detail branch looks up described.get('exhibit_now') before exhibit_now has been processed, so it falls back to the full subject_lock, then exhibit_now pastes it again. Also asset.view is wrapped as '(a close detail of ...)' producing 'a close detail (a close detail ...)'.","change":"seedance.py: bind references in two passes (non-detail assets first, then details pointing to the exhibit_now tag), or emit details as 'a close detail of the same subject as @ImageN' using the exhibit_now tag from seg.ref_assets regardless of order. Interim prompt rule in s3_script.md (Reference images paragraph): \"In ref_assets list the full current view of the exhibit before any detail of it, and write each asset's view as a short noun phrase ('rings near the bark'), not a sentence starting 'a close detail of'.\"","expected_effect":"Sequoia seg2 drops ~60 words to under 250 with no writer change; prevents the same failure on any future exhibit whose film opens on a detail."},{"title":"Every film ends on an imperative 'Look up (again)' or an equivalent instruction; endings command instead of land","severity":"high","evidence":"Hope final line 'Look up again. All of this — and I was young.' (S2 ending 'Look up again. That's a whale who hadn't finished growing.'); Sequoia 'Look up. Thirteen hundred years of rings, uncovered.'; Hope concept 2 'Look again and tell me what I'm worth'; Sophie concept 2 'Count them.' Only Sophie's chosen ending ('The rest of what you see is me.') lands without an instruction, and it is the strongest line in the three films.","root_cause":"Prompt (s2_concept.md): 'The film should answer the question they actually have and make them look up at the real object again at the end' is read literally as a line to say; METHOD.md's '画面让观众抬头' meant the image should do it.","change":"prompts/s2_concept.md, replace 'and make them look up at the real object again at the end.' with: \"and leave them wanting to check the real object themselves. The picture does that work: the last shot matches what they will see when they lower the phone. The last line must not tell them to look, count, notice or imagine (no 'look up', 'look again', 'see', 'count them'); it states the thought that will be in their head when they do look, in eight words or fewer, ideally a reframe of something visible ('The rest of what you see is me.').\" In prompts/s3_script.md after 'The ending completes that idea; it does not open a new one' add: \"The last line is never an instruction to the viewer; it is the turned thought, short enough to hang in the silence after it, and at least 1.5 seconds of picture and sound follow it.\"","expected_effect":"Endings shift from stage directions to payoff lines (Hope e.g. 'All of this, and I was young.'), raising the ending score toward 5 and removing the template feel that would be obvious when four or six films play back to back in a 2-minute demo."},{"title":"One-size template across three very different objects: 30 s, 2x3 shots, voiceover, sparse solo cello every time","severity":"medium","evidence":"All three chosen scripts are 30 s / 2 segments / 6 shots / realistic / voiceover. Music: hope 'Sparse solo cello with soft piano, slow and warm', sophie 'Sparse solo cello, slow low sustained notes, no percussion', sequoia 'Sparse low solo cello, slow sustained notes'. Sequoia expect.json requires 15 s ('thin pack, one headline fact'); S1 chose 30 s and S2/S3 padded it to six fact lines (narration words 47, 1.57 w/s) including a split fact ('That work revealed my many rings.' / 'They show how long I lived.'). Sequoia seg1 music 'holds a low note across the cut, no fade', violating the S3 rule to stop cleanly at the cut, and S4 missed it.","root_cause":"Prompts: s3_script.md gives no guidance on choosing music from the object's world, so the model defaults to the 'museum-poignant' cliche; s2_concept.md never asks whether the concept fits 15 s, it accepts S1's length (length decision itself is an S1 issue).","change":"prompts/s3_script.md, replace 'and a music direction. Describe one music style for the whole film and repeat it in both segments.' with: \"and a music direction drawn from the exhibit's own world and the concept's emotion, not a generic museum score: name instrumentation, tempo and one texture (for example sea: low synth swells and a single distant whale-like tone; quarry: dry plucked guitar, wind; workshop: no music, only brush and breath). Solo cello and 'soft piano' are banned as defaults; use them only if you can say why this exhibit needs them. 'No music' is often right for a 15-second film. Use one style for the whole film and repeat it word for word in both segments.\" prompts/s2_concept.md, append to the last paragraph: \"Before choosing, say whether the chosen concept fits in 15 seconds. If its idea needs only one fact and one image change, it is a 15-second film even if the material was rated 30; say so in your choice, and write the hook and ending lines to fit 25 words in total.\"","expected_effect":"Distinct sonic identity per exhibit (audio is generated by Seedance, so this is a free quality lever), and thin exhibits like the sequoia come out at 15 s with one tight idea, matching expect.json and the demo mix of 2x30 + 4x15."},{"title":"Announcer and filler lines spend scarce seconds: hook-level energy collapses after line 1","severity":"medium","evidence":"Hope seg2 opens with 'That number isn't the surprising part.' (signposts the twist, carries nothing) then says the twist three ways: 'I was a juvenile. Just a girl.' ... 'and I was young.' Sophie's hook 'Most Stegosaurus skeletons are found with parts missing.' is a textbook sentence, not a hook (S2 version 'come out of the ground in pieces' was better and was flattened in revision). Sequoia 'Beneath it, cloudy old varnish and wax.' is a list item. Sophie seg1 line 'on a hilltop in Wyoming, USA' adds geography that serves no turn.","root_cause":"Prompt (s3_script.md 'Write for the ear') covers diction but not line economy; revision feedback about truth wording ('incomplete' vs 'in pieces') pushed the writer to the pack's literal phrasing because nothing tells it to keep the spoken rhythm while fixing truth.","change":"prompts/s3_script.md, append to the 'Write for the ear' paragraph: \"Every line must earn its seconds: no line that only announces what is coming ('here's the surprising part'), no saying the same point twice in different words, no place name or date unless the turn needs it. Say the twist once, as late and as short as possible. Keep the concept's hook and ending lines word for word unless a truth problem forces a change; when it does, change the fewest words and keep the rhythm and the concrete image ('in pieces', not 'with parts missing', if the fact allows it).\"","expected_effect":"Frees 3-6 seconds per film for silence and the collision image; keeps the strongest concept lines alive through revisions; raises 'spoken' and 'hook' from 4 to 5."},{"title":"Concepts answer a second-order question or skip the living form the visitor came for","severity":"medium","evidence":"Sequoia chose 'Under the Dust', answering 'Why does the wood look so clean and glossy?', a question few visitors have; the age arrives only in the last line, and the 101-metre height (the scale shock) is dropped. 'You're Counting' answered the primary question and fitted 15 s. Sophie planned alive_restoration and sheet_living but the chosen film never shows her alive, so 'it was a real animal' (the emotional answer to 'is it real?') stays abstract. Hope is the only film whose concept answers the visitor's first question (size) and turns it (youth).","root_cause":"Prompt (s2_concept.md): 'Choose the concept that will work best for this visitor' plus 'most filmable' reasoning lets feasibility and novelty outrank the visitor's primary question; nothing asks the agent to rank visitor questions.","change":"prompts/s2_concept.md, insert before 'Write three concepts': \"First rank the visitor questions: the one almost everyone in front of this object has (usually size, age, or 'is it real?') comes first. At least two of your three concepts must answer that first question, and the third may answer a less common one only if its turn is clearly stronger. In your choice, name which question the film answers and why a visitor would rather have that answered than the first one, if it is not the first.\"","expected_effect":"Sequoia would land on age-through-rings at 15 s; future exhibits stop optimising for clever angles that miss what the visitor wonders, raising visitor_view from 4 to 5."},{"title":"Shot planning leans on risky references (multi-panel sheet as sole reference, shots under 3 s, rules smuggled into 'setting')","severity":"low","evidence":"Sequoia seg1 uses only sheet_realistic (a five-panel grid incl. dusty and half-cleaned states) for all three shots, inviting grid layouts or multiple slices. Sophie seg2 shot 2 is 18.0-20.5 s (2.5 s) though s3 says 'at least three seconds' (checks.MIN_SHOT_S = 2.5 lets it pass). Hope seg2 setting repeats 'the living whale and the skeleton are different states, never in the same shot, no morphing', duplicating the renderer's own warning (S4 flagged it).","root_cause":"Prompt (s3_script.md) does not say a character sheet is an identity aid only, nor that setting is scene only; check threshold disagrees with the prompt.","change":"prompts/s3_script.md, add to the Reference images paragraph: \"A character or reference sheet with several panels is never the only reference for a shot; pair it with a single-view image of the same state, or plan a single-view asset. 'setting' names the place and light only, in under 15 words; never put production rules in it, the request builder adds them.\" Set checks.MIN_SHOT_S = 3.0 to match the prompt.","expected_effect":"Fewer Seedance failures from grid/duplicate artefacts; a further 15-25 words saved in two-state segments; prompt and checks agree."}],"keep":["Truth discipline: all three chosen attempts reached truth 5 with claim kinds and fact ids, and no forbidden figures (no '38 years', no felling year, no bone counts, no percentages). Do not loosen the fact/visible/voice rules or the qualifier ban while pushing for more voice lines.","The Hope hook 'You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.' over a pull-back through the arch: the best moment across the three films; direct address built from what the photo shows. Keep sending the photo to S2/S3 and engine (e).","Sophie's ending 'The rest of what you see is me.' as the model ending: a reframe of what the visitor sees, no instruction, directly answers 'is it real?'.","S2 'Why this one' reasoning written from the visitor's side and the rule 'A concept that needs many facts to work is usually the wrong one' (Sophie and Hope choices both used 2-4 facts).","Cut design: putting the world jump at the segment cut with matched brightness (Hope bright glass roof -> sunlit sea surface) and near-still end frames.","Specific voice casting repeated verbatim in both segments (Hope's young woman with a gentle Irish lilt is a good, motivated choice).","Revision-edits-previous-draft rule: narration stayed stable across attempts and earlier truth fixes were not undone.","Pass/fail computed in code, not by the reviewer.","Closest to demo-ready: Sophie 'Almost Whole' (all scores >= 4, truth 5, one 254-word request 4 words over the cap; one trim from PASS). Hope 'Young Giant' is the better film (best hook, the only real image/line collision: youth spoken over a living whale, then over the adult-sized skeleton) and will overtake it once action/visual duplication and the announcer line are fixed. Sequoia is furthest: wrong length, literal captioning, a second-order question.","Single biggest craft gap: the words caption the pictures. Image and line are planned to show and say the same thing, there are almost no voice lines, and so the narrator's announced personality never appears."]},{"lens":"render","findings":[{"title":"The renderer pastes the full lock into every @Image binding, so the 250-word check fails on every run and the writer cannot fix it","severity":"high","evidence":"All three v1.2 runs fail mainly on length. Hope runs/1114-173547-hope-v1.2: seg1 350/293/271 words and seg2 410/355/325 over 3 attempts, never under 250. Sequoia seg2 is 360/291. Sophie seg1 is 254. The S4 reviews (05/07/09_s4_review) give the same cause every time: \"@Image1 is still described with the whole subject lock (35 words)\". In sequoia seg2, @Image1 and @Image2 each carry the full ~30-word lock (\"@Image1 is a close detail (a close detail of the slice's rings) of A large round cross-section...\"). checks.py:199 tells the writer to \"shorten shot visuals\", but S3 is told to copy the locks unchanged, so the writer has no lever. Research (practice.prompt_findings, image-first) says: do not re-describe what the reference already shows; spend the words on motion, camera and sound.","root_cause":"harness code: seedance.py render() binds exhibit_now/living_form/detail by pasting script.subject_lock / living_form_lock. The check message in checks.py points at the wrong field. CHANGELOG lesson: a blocking issue that survives every revision is a template bug.","change":"schemas.py Asset: add `label: str = Field(description=\"2-5 word name used in the video prompt, e.g. 'the whale skeleton', 'the living whale', 'the hall', 'the dusty slice'\")`. seedance.py: delete the `lock` dict and bind by label plus role:\nROLE = {\"exhibit_now\": \"copy its shape, pose and colour exactly; ignore its plain background\", \"living_form\": \"copy its body shape, colours and markings; ignore its background\", \"environment\": \"setting reference only: architecture, light and space\"}\nlabel = lambda a: _s(getattr(a, 'label', '') or a.view)\nbinding: exhibit_now/living_form -> f\"{tag} is {label(a)} ({ROLE[a.depicts]})\"; detail -> f\"{tag} is a close-up of {label(a)}, part of {described.get('exhibit_now', 'the exhibit')}\"; environment -> f\"{tag} is {label(a)} ({ROLE['environment']})\"; second view of an already-described subject -> f\"{tag} is another view of {described[a.depicts]}\".\nThe locks stay in S1b image prompts only (that is where identity is fixed). checks.py:199: change the message to f\"segment {i} request is {n} words (cap {cap}); longest parts: bindings {b}, shots {s}, voice {v}, sound {so}\", using a word breakdown returned by render() as `prompt_parts`. Set MAX_PROMPT_WORDS = 220.","expected_effect":"Removes 30-70 words per request (two locks in Hope seg2, two in sequoia seg2). Combined with the shot de-duplication below, typical requests come out at about 160-200 words, so the length failure (one of the blocking issues in every v1.2 run) goes away without any writer change. Identity comes from the images, which is what Seedance actually copies."},{"title":"Each shot is printed as action plus visual, and the two restate each other; Seedance gets the same instruction twice","severity":"high","evidence":"Hope seg1 shot 2: \"Camera glides from the jaw tips along the spine toward the tail. From jaw tips along the spine toward the tail.\" Sequoia seg2: \"slow push-in from the bark edge toward the centre. Light glides. Cinnamon-red bark giving way to...\"; \"static. Light settles.\". Sophie seg1: \"slow push-in. fine dust drifts in the wind.\" (lower-case after the full stop). S4 flags the duplication in every Hope and Sequoia review.","root_cause":"schema plus prompt: ShotSpec has action, and also visual 'what is on screen, at most 25 words', with no rule against overlap. The renderer prints both, joined by '. ', without deduplicating or capitalising.","change":"schemas.py ShotSpec.visual description: \"Optional, at most 15 words: composition, light or colour that the action does not already say. Never repeat the action or describe the exhibit's shape/colour (the reference shows it). Empty is fine.\" S3 prompt, replace \"(at most 25 words per shot visual)\" with: \"action is the one motion in the shot, written as a full clause ('the camera pulls back through a terracotta arch until the whole skeleton fits'); visual adds only light, colour or composition that the action does not say, at most 15 words, often empty.\" seedance.py shot line: body = _s(s.action); if s.visual and _overlap(s.visual, s.action) < 0.5: body += '; ' + _s(s.visual)[0].lower() + _s(s.visual)[1:], where _overlap = share of content words of visual found in action. Print f\"{lead}{refs}: {_s(s.framing)}, {_s(s.camera)}; {body}.\" so the clause after the camera is never a sentence fragment starting in lower case.","expected_effect":"About 10-25 words saved per shot, and one clear instruction per shot (camera then action), which follows the official per-shot order (camera, then action, then space, then audio). Lower risk of Seedance reading the repetition as two separate events or an extra cut."},{"title":"Voiceover lines sit in one block after all the shots, far down the prompt and not tied to any shot","severity":"high","evidence":"Every request puts every quoted line after the third shot, ~180-250 words in: \"Voiceover, unseen narrator, A young woman in her early twenties, ...: \\\"You had to go up a floor...\\\" \\\"From jaw to tail...\\\"\". Research: the first instructions are followed most reliably, so front-load the binding and the narration (verified implications_for_script). fal and the official guide: keep lines short and split speech across cuts, one sentence per cut (\"Shot 1 line: ... Cut to detail. Shot 2 line: ...\"). The per-line t_start (1.2 s, 8.0 s) and the head-silence and tail rules are never passed to Seedance, so it may start speaking at 0 s or pile all lines into the first shot. Lower-case 'A young woman' after a comma also produces a malformed sentence.","root_cause":"seedance.py: narration is rendered as one concatenated block after the shots. Line timing is in the schema but not in the request.","change":"seedance.py: (1) put a narrator line right after the bindings/style: f\"Narration: an off-screen voiceover, {_s(script.voice)[0].lower()+_s(script.voice)[1:]}. No one on screen speaks or moves their lips. No speech in the first second; the last words end a second before the clip ends.\" For on_screen: f\"The {living label} speaks on screen in {voice}.\" (2) Give each line to the shot in which it starts: shot_of = max(i for i,s in enumerate(seg.shots) if s.t_start <= l.t_start + 1e-6), and append to that shot: f' Voiceover: \"{l.text.strip()}\"' (on_screen: f' It says: \"...\"'). (3) Delete the old trailing narration block. S3 prompt, add to Narration: \"Start each line inside the shot it belongs to and keep it within that shot; one or two short sentences per shot.\" checks.py: flag a line whose spoken end (t_start + words/2.5) passes its shot's t_end by more than 0.5 s.","expected_effect":"The narration moves into the first ~60% of the prompt and lines up with the cuts, which is the pattern Seedance documents for timing speech. That gives better word-for-word delivery and sync, less chance of a line being dropped at the end of a long prompt, and the one-second head and tail silence is requested rather than only checked."},{"title":"Multi-panel character sheets and multi-state sheets are planned as Seedance references (twin risk), and the state shown contradicts its binding","severity":"high","evidence":"S1b prompt: the character sheet \"is the identity reference for every shot that shows the living form\". Hope 02_s1b_assets plans sheet_realistic (six whales, a redraw of a labelled sheet) and reuses the existing labelled id, which S4 marked blocking: \"<sheet_realistic> could therefore pull the version with the HOPE/FRONT/CALM labels... six copies of the whale, which invites duplicates\". Sequoia seg1 passes sheet_realistic (a five-panel grid: front, side, 3/4, dusty, half-cleaned) as its only @Image1, bound as \"glossy warm reddish-brown cut face\", while the shots ask for thick grey dust and a half-clean face. Research (official, practice.prompt_findings): \"multi-view or three-view sheets get read as different subjects and cause duplicates ('twins')\"; object multi-view is fine only when each image is one view.","root_cause":"S1b prompt wording (sheets as video references, no per-state images, ids reused) plus seedance.py (binds by depicts and never by the asset's own state).","change":"S1b prompt, replace the sentence \"It is the identity reference for every shot that shows the living form.\" with: \"A sheet is never sent to the video model. From it, plan one single-view crop, the full side view alone on the plain background, with id '<sheet id>_side'. That crop is the identity reference for living-form shots.\" Add: \"If the story needs the exhibit in another state (dusty, half-cleaned, in the ground, being excavated), plan one separate single image per state, same angle and framing as the exhibit-now view, named for the state (slice_dusty, slice_half_clean).\" Add: \"Never reuse an existing image's id for a new image; a fixed version gets a new id (sheet_realistic_clean).\" S3 prompt, Reference images: \"Never use a character sheet or any multi-panel image as a reference; use its single-view crop. A shot that shows the exhibit in another state uses that state's image.\" checks.py: problem if a ref_asset's S1b kind is character_sheet, or if the id matches an existing asset with has_text_labels=true.","expected_effect":"Removes the most likely visual failure Seedance has (duplicate whales or slices in frame), stops the labelled sheet leaking text, and makes sequoia seg1 renderable: the dusty and half-clean shots get references that show those states instead of a glossy grid."},{"title":"No in-place view of the exhibit: S3 invents empty-hall plates, and the final 'match the visitor's view' shot has to composite a grey-background subject into a hall with the subject removed","severity":"medium","evidence":"S1b's standard set has no environment image. S3 generates hall_balcony (\"Hintze Hall from first-floor balcony level, without the whale\"), hintze_balcony (\"the empty upper balcony\"), quarry_fragments and excavation_bones with no S1b rules. The binding then reads \"@Image2 is Hintze Hall... without the whale\", which can be read as 'no whale in the shot'. Sophie S4 (05_s4_review): \"The only reference is a studio shot on a light grey seamless background... the model may render a grey void instead.\" The concept's ending is always 'the real exhibit from the visitor's photo angle', yet the visitor's photo (which already shows exactly that) is never a reference.","root_cause":"S1b prompt (standard set is missing an in-situ view) plus seedance.py (environment bound by view text, with no role).","change":"S1b prompt, add item 5: \"In-place view (when a visitor photo exists): the visitor's photo cleaned up, with the same angle and framing, the exhibit in its hall, glare removed, any signs and readable text removed, people kept small or removed. It is the reference for the final shot the visitor matches when they look up. Without a photo, plan an environment image of the place with the exhibit in it, never an 'empty' version.\" Also add: \"Give every image a 2-5 word label.\" S3 prompt: \"The final shot uses the in-place view as its reference.\" seedance.py: environment binding role \"setting reference only: architecture, light and space\" (see finding 1), and never print 'without the X'.","expected_effect":"The ending shot is anchored to a real image of the visitor's own view, so the screen-to-object match works and there is no grey-void or 'hall without whale' ambiguity. The plates S3 used to invent become planned assets that follow the no-text, no-people rules."},{"title":"Sound block: per-shot effects are detached from their shots, the join can be empty ('; ;'), music is restated per segment and can be told to run across the cut, and end_frame is never sent","severity":"medium","evidence":"Hope final seg1 per S4 09: \"the Sound line contains an empty '; ;'\" (empty sfx list). The Sound line comes after the shots as \"ambience; sfx; music; ending clause\", e.g. \"Sound: Large stone hall: ...; a child's distant gasp...; Sparse solo cello...; ends on one held cello note...\". Sequoia seg1 music: \"holds a low note across the cut, no fade\". S4 (05) asked for this, which contradicts S3's rule and is impossible across two separate generations. Research: narration clips often end with clicks or cut-off noise, so fade in post. Segment.end_frame (\"Near-still low-angle view of pale ribs...\") appears in no request.","root_cause":"seedance.py (sound join, end_frame unused), schema (music lives per segment, no per-shot sfx field), S4 prompt (does not know the cut constraint).","change":"schemas.py: Script.music_style: str (\"one music style for the whole film, or 'no music'\"), Segment.music_cue: str (\"how the music opens or ends in this segment\"), ShotSpec.sfx: str = \"\" (\"at most one sound tied to this shot's action\"). seedance.py: after each shot's action, add f\"; sound: {_s(s.sfx)}\" when set. Sound line: \"Sound: \" + \"; \".join(p for p in [_s(a.ambience), *map(_s, a.sfx)] if p) + f\". Music: {music_style}, {cue}.\" (or \"No music.\"). On the last shot of every non-final segment, add f\"End on a near-still image: {_s(seg.end_frame)}; the music has faded out by then.\" S4 prompt, add: \"The two segments are separate generations joined by a hard cut: never ask for music, speech or a camera move to continue across it; segment 1's music ends or fades before its last second.\" S3 prompt Sound paragraph, add: \"Never let the music hold across the cut.\"","expected_effect":"Effects land on the action they belong to (Seedance's own per-shot order), with no malformed lines. The music style is identical in both requests by construction. Segment 1 ends on a still, quiet frame that hard-cuts cleanly, and the reviewer stops undoing that rule."},{"title":"Setting and state lines are duplicated, and the states warning does not name which images it means","severity":"medium","evidence":"Hope seg2 says it twice: \"These are different states of the subject: never show them in the same shot...\" and, in the setting, \"...back to Hintze Hall balcony level; the living whale and the skeleton are different states, never in the same shot, no morphing.\" S4 (07) flags it. Settings also carry two places joined with 'then' (\"Open sea just below a sunlit surface, then one hard cut back to...\").","root_cause":"S3 prompt (no length or content rule for setting) plus seedance.py (generic states sentence).","change":"S3 prompt Structure: \"setting: the place in at most 12 words; never restate rules (states, no morphing, no text) because the request builder adds them.\" seedance.py: replace the generic states sentence with one that names the tags: f\"{tag_living} ({label_living}) and {tag_now} ({label_now}) never appear in the same shot: hard cut between them, no morphing or transformation.\" Also change the style line to f\"Style: {look}.\" with look = \"photorealistic documentary footage, natural light\" (drop 'cinematic', a mood word the fal guide says renders nothing).","expected_effect":"Saves 15-30 words and gives Seedance an unambiguous rule tied to specific images, which lowers the risk of a fleshy skeleton or a morph in the mixed-state segment."},{"title":"The constraint line uses generic wording; use the official phrasing and name the subject","severity":"low","evidence":"Every request ends \"No subtitles, no on-screen text or lettering, no logos, no watermark, no duplicate copies of the subject.\" Research (official appendix, reported second-hand): 'Keep it subtitle-free; avoid generating any text or subtitles'; the anti-twin line is 'Keep only a single corresponding [object] in the same frame'. The model adds subtitles unprompted, especially when there is narration.","root_cause":"seedance.py constraint string","change":"seedance.py: parts.append(f\"Keep it subtitle-free: no subtitles, captions, letters, numbers, logos or watermark anywhere. Only one {label of the main subject} in any frame.\"). Keep it as the last line.","expected_effect":"Constraints follow the documented wording and are specific ('only one whale skeleton'). This is cheap and lowers the subtitle and twin risk, which is highest when narration is on."},{"title":"Voice continuity across the two 15 s generations rests only on repeating the text; add an optional voice-chain A/B","severity":"low","evidence":"The voice description is repeated verbatim (good), but 2.0 has no seed and community reports say voices are 'recognisably similar, not identical'. Research (official tip, practice.audio): pass the same voice sample as @Audio1 and say 'in the voice of @Audio1'; one practical option is clip 1's narration as @Audio1 for clip 2. Unverified whether @Audio1 is copied as content or used only as timbre (still_open).","root_cause":"seedance.py has no audio_urls path","change":"seedance.py: env flag SEEDANCE_VOICE_CHAIN=1. When it is on and index > 0, add \"audio_urls\": [\"<segment1_voice_clip>\"] (≤15 s, voice only) and change the narrator sentence to f\"Narration: an off-screen voiceover in the voice of @Audio1 ({voice}).\" Keep the default off until an A/B on the cheap tier (mini, 480p, 5 s) shows whether the clip is only matched for timbre and not replayed.","expected_effect":"Gives a measured route to a consistent narrator while Seedance still produces all the audio, without committing to an unverified behaviour."},{"title":"Camera angle and distance in shots must fit the reference and the shot length","severity":"low","evidence":"Hope seg2 shot 1, \"wide, looking up from below\", uses alive_underwater, which is a three-quarter side view. S4 (07): \"Seedance tends to drift from the reference, or to warp the whale's shape, when the angle differs this much.\" Hope seg1 shot 2 drifts jaw to tail along 25 m in 5 s (S4 09: \"forces a fast lateral move... motion blur\").","root_cause":"S3 prompt wording","change":"S3 prompt Shots paragraph, add: \"Frame each shot within about 45 degrees of the angle its reference image shows; for a different angle, the asset plan needs a new view. A camera move covers only what can be travelled slowly in the shot's length (a drift over the skull in five seconds, not jaw to tail).\"","expected_effect":"Fewer warped subjects and fewer fast moves that Seedance renders as blur or as an unplanned cut."}],"keep":["Separation of what the agent decides (schemas.Script) from how it is written for Seedance (seedance.py), and S4 seeing the rendered request: this is how every template bug in v1, v1.1 and v1.2 was found.","bytedance/seedance-2.0/reference-to-video, duration as a string \"4\"-\"15\", 720p, 16:9, generate_audio true, no seed, no 1080p, no timecodes in the prompt (only a soft hint).","@ImageN in upload order, each image bound once, tags reused in shots; at most 4 references per segment.","'Shot 1 ... Cut to shot N' in event order, 2-3 shots of at least 3 s per 15 s segment, exactly one camera move and one action per shot, and the exhibit itself never moves.","Spoken lines in English double quotes, numbers and dates in words, 2.5 words/s, at least 0.8-1 s of opening silence, the last line ending at least 1 s before the segment ends, and the checks that enforce this.","Identical voice description repeated verbatim in both segments, and the single music style repeated in both.","Hard cut between the two generations, with the big now/then jump placed at the cut, and the end_frame idea (it now needs rendering).","The v1.1/v1.2 rule that different subject states (living form vs exhibit) are bound separately, never share a shot, and carry an explicit 'no morphing' line; the checks.py mixed-state check.","Explicit sound brief in every request, with 'no music' when none is wanted.","Constraint line last: subtitle-free, no text, logos or watermark, no duplicate subject.","S1b rules: one subject alone, even light, no text, people or glass in reference prompts; existing images reused by id; reconstruction marked as artistic.","Pass/fail computed in code, best attempt kept, and the lesson that a blocking issue surviving every revision points at the template."]}]
  
  Blind judging (in the prompts, the v0 script was labelled A and the v1 script B):
  [{"per_criterion":[{"criterion":"Hook (first 2-3 s)","better":"B","why":"B opens on the visitor's own action: \"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.\" It speaks to the person holding the phone and matches the balcony photo. A's \"Twenty-five metres of me.\" is a bare number that only restates the size the visitor is already looking at. B's line is long, though, and runs to about 5 s."},{"criterion":"Answers what a visitor wonders","better":"tie","why":"Both answer 'how big is it?' and then turn it into 'it was only young'. A also covers when and where it died and why it has its name. That answers more questions, but it buries the turn."},{"criterion":"One idea","better":"B","why":"B holds to one idea throughout: huge, yet young (\"All of this — and I was young\"). A piles up length, youth, tonnage, life at sea, the date, the sandbar, the pose and the naming. Its ending (\"a future you can still shape\") switches to a second, sustainability idea."},{"criterion":"Counterpoint","better":"A","why":"A's lines add things the picture can't show: \"Four and a half tonnes, just in bone\", \"Out there, I was blue, not bone\" and the 1891 date. In Segment 1, B's lines describe what is already in view (\"I measure just over twenty-five metres\"). B only adds hidden information with \"juvenile\"."},{"criterion":"Truth","better":"B","why":"A uses the exact qualifier the quality bar bans: \"not finished growing\". It also shows a \"lone\" whale (the banned 'alone') and a flank \"lying motionless\" (implies death). It invents stained glass that is not in the facts, and \"I'm still diving\" leans on the unsourced dive pose. Everything B claims is sourced: \"just over twenty-five metres\" is 25.2 m, and \"a juvenile. Just a girl.\" is accurate. B's \"step back\" assumes a little about what the visitor did."},{"criterion":"Renderability","better":"A","why":"Each of A's shots has one move, one action and detailed visual descriptions. B also keeps one move per shot, but its prompts are thin: \"The living whale glides slowly overhead\" never describes the whale, and the 6-11 s shot repeats itself. A video model may render a generic whale. Neither script puts text on screen. A's harbour shots each hold a single subject state."},{"criterion":"Spoken quality and timing","better":"B","why":"A crams eight lines into 30 s, mostly fragments, and the delivery feels list-like. B has five lines with room to breathe and a clean setup and payoff (\"That number isn't the surprising part.\"). Its weak spots are the long opening line and the VO at 19.0 s starting just before the cut at 19.5 s."},{"criterion":"Ending that lands and returns eye to object","better":"B","why":"B's last shot is framed from the balcony to match the visitor's photo, under \"Look up again. All of this — and I was young.\" That sends the eye straight back to the real skeleton and closes the single idea. A's stained-glass close-up and its line about the name are moving, but they bring in a new idea and an invented visual. Its \"Look up\" comes in the third-to-last shot, not at the end."}],"overall":"B","confidence":"medium","best_line_overall":"\"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.\" (Script B)","weakest_point_of_winner":"The Segment 1 lines mostly restate what the photo already shows (the length). The underwater shots are underspecified for a video model (\"The living whale glides...\" with no description of the whale), so the reveal shot could render as a generic whale.","reasoning":"Script A is the more cinematic and textured of the two: the tonnage line, \"blue, not bone\" and the Wexford date all add real counterpoint. But it fails the two bars a brand film for a museum can't afford to fail. It breaks truth: it uses the explicitly banned \"not finished growing\", adds a \"lone\" whale and a motionless flank that imply death, and invents stained glass. It also tries to hold too many ideas, ending on a sustainability pivot rather than its own turn. Script B is leaner, and its visuals are thinner. Still, it hooks on the visitor's actual balcony moment and sticks to one idea (huge, yet young). It stays inside the facts and ends on a shot matching the visitor's photo, which sends the eye back to the real skeleton. A director would fix B's thin shot prompts sooner than A's factual and structural problems."},{"per_criterion":[{"criterion":"Hook (first 2-3 s)","better":"B","why":"B's line at 1.2s, \"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in,\" speaks to what this visitor just did on the balcony. A's \"Twenty-five metres of me\" is a bare number. A's shot is also from the hall floor, not from where the visitor stood."},{"criterion":"Answers what a visitor wonders","better":"tie","why":"Both answer 'how big is it?' and then give the surprise that she was young. A also says how she came to be here, with the date and Wexford, which a visitor might wonder about, but A has to crowd its lines to fit it in."},{"criterion":"One idea","better":"B","why":"B keeps to one idea, 'all this, and I was young'. A piles up length, juvenile, 4.5 tonnes, 'blue, not bone', date, place, 'still diving' and the meaning of the name: seven beats in 30 s."},{"criterion":"Counterpoint","better":"A","why":"A's lines and pictures each add something: 'Out there, I was blue, not bone' plays over the living whale, and the date plays over the harbour. B's \"From jaw to tail, I measure...\" is spoken over a jaw-to-tail drift, which only repeats the picture. B's underwater whale does illustrate 'juvenile' well."},{"criterion":"Truth","better":"B","why":"A breaks the bar outright with \"not finished growing\", which is a forbidden added qualifier. Its logline adds \"cut short\", and the shot of her lying 'motionless' on the sand implies a death the facts don't state. B stays inside the facts: 'just over twenty-five metres' and 'a juvenile. Just a girl.'"},{"criterion":"Renderable","better":"B","why":"B uses six simple shots with one move each. A is mostly fine, but its 9.5-15s shot has two moves ('drift alongside, then gentle tilt up') and its 22.5-26s crane needs faces and then a reveal. A also has more shots and more complex scenes to generate."},{"criterion":"Spoken quality and timing","better":"B","why":"A's lines come every 2-3 s ('Twenty-five metres of me' at 1.2, then a new line at 3.4), which is rushed and list-like. B is paced in a natural, conversational way, though it leaves a long gap with no voice from about 10 s to 16 s."},{"criterion":"Ending sends eye back","better":"B","why":"B ends on a shot matching the visitor's own photo from the balcony with \"Look up again. All of this — and I was young.\" That is a clean payoff on its one idea, though 'look up' slightly misfits a visitor who is on the balcony. A's \"Look up. I'm still diving\" is strong, but the line about the name that follows piles a second ending on top and weakens it."}],"overall":"B","confidence":"medium","best_line_overall":"\"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.\" (Script B)","weakest_point_of_winner":"The lines are thin and in places don't add to the picture. \"From jaw to tail, I measure just over twenty-five metres\" just describes a jaw-to-tail camera drift, and \"That number isn't the surprising part\" is a filler line. It also leaves about 6 s with no voice.","reasoning":"A has richer material and some lovely lines ('Out there, I was blue, not bone', 'Look up. I'm still diving'). But it breaks the truth bar in two ways. 'Not finished growing' is the exact kind of added qualifier the bar names. The motionless-on-sandbar shot, with 'cut short' in its logline, implies a death the facts don't give. It also crams seven ideas into 30 s with rushed VO. B is the script you can safely ship: its hook is tied to where the visitor stood, it has one clear idea, its facts stay accurate and its ending returns to the visitor's own view. Its weakness is lean, slightly redundant narration."},{"per_criterion":[{"criterion":"Hook (first 2-3 s)","better":"B","why":"B's \"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.\" names exactly what this visitor just did on the first-floor balcony, which makes it personal and immediate. A's \"Twenty-five metres of me.\" is clean and fast but generic. It is a number, and it does not acknowledge the visitor."},{"criterion":"Answers what a visitor wonders","better":"A","why":"A covers size, age, weight, the date and place of the stranding, and the name, so it touches every likely question, including \"why is it called Hope?\". B only covers size and youth and never says her name or where she came from, which is a real gap for a visitor standing in front of Hope."},{"criterion":"One idea","better":"B","why":"B builds one turn: the length (\"just over twenty-five metres\"), then \"That number isn't the surprising part\", then \"I was a juvenile. Just a girl\", then the closing \"All of this — and I was young.\" A packs in eight separate facts (length, girlhood, tonnage, colour, date, sandbar, diving, naming), and the closing Hope line is a second idea grafted on, not the payoff of the first."},{"criterion":"Counterpoint","better":"A","why":"A's lines mostly add what the picture can't show: \"Four and a half tonnes, just in bone\" over the ribs, the 1891 date over the harbour, \"Out there, I was blue, not bone.\" B's \"From jaw to tail, I measure...\" plays over a drift from jaw to tail, so the words just describe the shot. B also leaves about 8 s of silence (8-16 s) over the bones."},{"criterion":"Truth / factual integrity","better":"B","why":"A's \"I was a girl, not finished growing\" adds exactly the qualifier the bar forbids (\"finished growing\"); the sources say only juvenile. A's visuals of a \"lone\" whale and a flank \"lying motionless\", plus \"cut short\" in its logline, push toward alone and died. B stays within the sources: juvenile, \"just a girl\", \"just over twenty-five metres\". Both lean on the mid-dive pose, but the brief states it as what the visitor sees, and A goes further with \"I'm still diving.\""},{"criterion":"Renderability","better":"A","why":"A's shot prompts are rich and self-contained, with one move each and a defined subject (a slender young blue whale, ivory-cream bone, terracotta arches). B's prompts are thin and repetitive (\"From jaw tips along the spine toward the tail.\"), and \"the living whale\" is never described for the model. Neither has text on screen or two subject states in one shot. A's coloured stained-glass light is an unsourced visual detail, but it is only a visual."},{"criterion":"Spoken quality and timing","better":"B","why":"B's five lines are conversational and well spaced, and the line \"That number isn't the surprising part\" works as a spoken pivot. A's eight lines come in quick bursts (about one every 3 s), which feels crowded for 30 s and leaves fragments like \"Morning, on a sandbar in Wexford Harbour.\" B's long silent stretch is its timing weakness."},{"criterion":"Ending that lands and returns the eye","better":"B","why":"B closes on a wide from the first-floor balcony that matches the visitor's photo, with \"Look up again. All of this — and I was young.\", which sends the eye straight back to the real object from where they stand. A's \"They named me Hope — for a future you can still shape.\" is resonant and well sourced, but it ends on a stained-glass close-up of the skull rather than the visitor's view, and it changes subject at the last moment."}],"overall":"B","confidence":"medium","best_line_overall":"\"You had to go up a floor and step back just to fit all of me in.\" (B)","weakest_point_of_winner":"B leaves out the name Hope and the Wexford/1891 origin, both sourced and both things a visitor would wonder about. Its middle (8-16 s) is silent, and its one length line just describes the jaw-to-tail shot. Its shot prompts are also underspecified for a video model.","reasoning":"Judged strictly on factual integrity, A breaks an explicit rule. \"Not finished growing\" is a forbidden qualifier, and A's visuals and logline (\"lone\", \"lying motionless\", \"cut short\") add the implications of being alone and having died. A is richer and better at counterpoint and renderability, but it scatters eight facts and has no single spine. B is narrower and has weaknesses: it never says the name Hope, has a silent gap, and gives thin prompts. But it builds one clear idea (huge, yet young), opens with a hook that speaks to this visitor's actual balcony viewpoint, ends on the visitor's own framing, and says nothing beyond the sources. For a museum that will not let interpretation go beyond the evidence, B is the safer and more coherent piece. Adding the Hope line could be a revision."}]
